AMATEUR BOWLERS.

In connectiOn with the Warrington and Dis- trict Association the games on Saturday were of a very interesting character, and one was glad t,o see that the Liberal Club team were so successful by winning over the Stag team from Walton by 45 points. The victors were by far the better team, both at home and 'away, and they should go far in this competition now. The semi-finals for the Championship and the Consolation Competition will be played on July 16th, and the finals the following Saturday.

===== 0002601_19140620_art0248.txt | included=true | included =====
41, 0 WORKS' ASSOCIATION.

Great excitement prevailed on the Horse and Jockey Green on Saturday, and this, or rather the keenness of the games, is due in a large measure to the handicapping system with which Mr. Patrick expresses himself as highly de- lighted. All day there was remarkable bowling, two tied matches being recorded. The second round was concluded and the third round com- menced under ideal conditions, the green being in fine condition. Richmond's (Academy-street) team had little difficulty in disposing of the lands' No. 2 men by 23 points, for nine of their players returned winning cards. W. Pilkerton (6), the Richmond's skipper, played well in his game with J. Woods (3), over whom he won by two points, after having led 13-7 at one time. W. Berry (4) was in no way discour- aged when his opponent, W. Charnock (7), ran to 10-7, but went on playing steadily until he was rewarded with a three points' win. E. Gleave (4) led 10-8 against T. Hardman (3), but at the 14th end 12 all was put up. Gleave went to 14-13, but, peeling on ,the next end, Hard- man sent a splendid couple down to win by one point. - - - - -

LEAD WORKS' SUCCESS. Crosfield's (Cheshire) had to bow the knee to the men from the Mersey White Lead Works, but, although beaten, they were in no way dis- graced, unless a defeat by four points can be called such. Both teams had nothing at which. to grumble in the handicap, for there was not a man who had less than four points start. Curiously enough, the Crosfield's men won more of the individual games than their opponents, but their majorities were not so large.

Third Round. A ONE-POINT VICTORY. It was a great battle between P. and K. Rolling Mills and Rylands' No. 3, for the former only won by a single point, the final result being very undecided up to the last players leaving the green. Amongst the meritorious wins recorded on either side was that of W. Phillips (6) over W. Whittaker (4), for the Rylands' man ran up to 14-11 at the 14th end. However, Whittaker peeled at the 17th end, but, unable to follow his own block, he lost by the odd chalk. J. Dagnall (5) against L. Bate (5) looked a very likely winner when he led 12-6 at the seventh end, but here Bate got the jack, ran his woods well, peeled at 12, and went to 14-12.. A double from Da.gnall made the scores equal again, and Bate just managed to land the winner on the next length. The other games were also of an interesting character.

TWO TIES. Walker's No. 1 tied with Howley Tannery, and theznatch between Fletcher's and the Assus- ance Agents resulted similarly. Walker's played a, slightly better game than their opponents in some instances, but, taken all round, the men were very evenly matched. Spencer (7) and Lawton (5) peeled at 13, and Spencer had to play some good woods to win by one point on the 13th end. Roberts (6) and Ditchfield (4) played a similar game, for 11-11 was booked at the 10th end, and subsequently Roberts only won by a couple. After tieing with 149, the Assurance Agents—those men with leisure moments in which to practice—beat the men from Fletcher's by 24 points, which was a re- markable win. T. Boyd (4), for the Agents, won a fine game over H. Green (5) by one point in 16 ends, after peeling at 6,8; 11 and 14.

===== 0002601_19140620_art0249.txt | included=true | included =====
LAST TWO MATCHES.

The final matches of the day were quite in- comparable so far as the results go, for Rich- mond's won over Mersey White Lead Works by 22 points, whereas Crosfield's Joiners only beat Cheshire Lines No. 2by four points. Each team won six individual games, but Crosfield's were slightly the better team, and no one could have hoped for such .41. better termination to such a capital match than ,their small win.

he 0 INDIVIDUAL MERIT.

Entries for the above competition will close to-day and a start will be made next Saturday, after the semi-final in the Works' Competition has been decided.

C 0 0:0 0

A NEW LEAGUE.

We are glad to note that a 111unicipal Bowling Greens League has been formed, and the first two matches will be played on Wednesday, be- tween Liverpool-road and Latchford No. 2, and Latchford No. Iv. St. Elphin's. An average of 60 members per green handed in their names, and it was decided that each team should be composed of 24 players, 12 to play at home and 12 away the same evening. Mr. J. H. Knowles has been elected secretary and treasurer, and Mr. J. E. Eaton the chairman.

0 e COO THANKS.

The secretary and committee of the Liberal Bowling Club desire to thank- the vice-presi- dents of the club for their generous response to Vice-presidents' Handicap in subscribing to the prizes. •_

1.0.0. F., 11.1. U., BOWLERS.

" Crosiield " _Cupftvon by A. E.

Thorniley.

Glorious weather favoured the 12th annual bowling main in connection with the "Prince of Wales" Lodge of the above Order, which was held at the Cat and Lion, Stretton, on Saturday. There were 71 entries and each player tried his utmost to win the much-coveted trophy given by Bro. A. H. Crosfield, and the 16 other prizes, which will be presented at the Lodge to-day. After many strenuous games had been played, Bros. J. Hitcbmough and A. E. Thorniley quali- fied for the final. Each player bowled well, but Thornfley went ahead and triumphed. He is thus the winner of the gold medal, and the holder, for twelve months, of the cup. Bro. T. Reddish refereed the games in a creditable fashion.

The Mayor sent a letter apologising for his inability to be present and wishing the main , every success, as indeed it was under the super- vision of P.P.G.M. T. P. Bradshaw (chairman), Bros. G. T. Freeman, J. Massey, T. Wadsworth, H. Farrar, E. Clayton, H. Marsh, T. Reddish, A. E. Price, and F. J. Coakley (secretary), who were responsible for the arrangements. The details were:—

THE EXAMINER SATURDAY, JUNE 10, 1914.

CRICKET.

A RUNAWAY VICTORY.

Warrington Were in a happy mood on Satur- day, when at home they were opposed. by Boughton Hall. The visiting team were the first to wield the willow, but their united efforts only realised the meagre score of one over the half-century, of which S. W. H. Welsby obtained 22 without being dismissed. Four of the players were unable to score, whilst it speaks well for the home team that not a single extra was recorded against them.

Warrington's reply was decisive, for ydth only four wickets down 211 were scored. "Stan" Partridge batted well, and his 87, the highest score of the day, was compiled in a masterly fashion. The second highest scorer was W. Richardson, who was responsible for 78 ere he was bowled by C. A. Bodner. Hannam and J. C. Bradburn both added 11 to the aggregate, whilst S. Baxter made seven (not out).

The byes in Warrington's favour totalled 17. Hannam (the home team professional) and W. Bennett were the only ones to bowl against Boughton Hall, and so effective were they that Hannam secured five wickets for 22 runs and W. Bennett five wickets for 29 runs. For the visitors H. L. Churton's average was the best, he securing one wicket, which cost 34 runs.

The Warrington second eleven, who visited the Boughton ground, came home victorious. As was the case with the senior team, they were the smarter side in the field and dismissed the home team for 105. There were only three play- ers who could withstand the Warringtonians' bowling, and between them they were respon- sible for 78 runs. For the visitors the highest score was 18, a figure that was reached by J. Thompson, who was unfortunately run out when he was just settling down, W. V. Turton, and also by "Mr. Extras." Warrington's aggregate was 124 for the loss of ten wickets.

Sankey visited St. Helens on Saturday, where they were defeated by St. Thomas' by ten runs. Forster, for the Sankey players, compiled 26 before he was caught.

The Moore and Daresbury team found that Barnton, their Saturday visitors, were deter- mined to win, and soon gave up the ghost. They could only muster 32, against their opponents' score of one short of the century.

The Highfield Tannery eleven visited Grappen- hall on Saturday and proved the better team by 113-57.

In the Warrington and District Competition the Conservatives and Rylands' Recreation were in opposition at Gorsey-lane, when the former won by 71-59. For Rylands', Waywell took seven wickets for 30 runs.

Bold-street Wesleyans met the Padgate Wes- leyan team on the Bold-street ground. Winning the toss, the homesters batted first, and, owing to good batting by W. Stringer (20), P. A. Day (18) and W. Cooper (13), reached a total of 69. Owing to the bowling of W. Stringer for Bolel- street, Padgate could only score 24, Bold-strcet thus winning by 45 runs. Stringer, who through- out the innings maintained a fine length, secured eight wickets for 15 runs, Weaver tak- ing the remaining two for

ASSOCIATION.

CHESHIRE FOOTBALL ASSOCIATION Annual Meeting at Crewe.

Mr. E. Case presided at the annual meeting of the Cheshire Football Association, which was held at the Royal Hotel, Crewe, on Saturday. There was a large attendance of representatives from the affiliated associations. The balance sheet submitted by Mr. G. S. N. Hull showed that the total receipts, including a balance of .C 482 48. 11d, brought forward from the previous year, amounted to £7Bl 13s. 3d., whilst the expenditure had been £3OB 19s. 9d., leaving a balance of £472 138. 6d. The gross gate receipts of the senior cup competition amounted to £356 I7s. 10d•; percentage of gate receipts, preliminary ties, ...t62 16s. 6d.; entry fees, .k.B 58. making a total income on senior cup account of £427 19s. 4d. After expenses had ; been paid the balance of £164 us. 11d. was car- ried forward to the general account mentioned above.

was 12s. 10d.; and The amateur cup account showed that the percentage of gate receipts from preliminary ties the gross gate receipts from semi-final and final ties £B7 4s. 9d. Medals cost ..tll lls.; semi-final and final tie expenses absorbed .C 36 7s. 5d.; and the shares of gates paid to the four clubs competing in the semi- final and final stages amounted to £.30 10s. 4d. Mr. Hull explained that there was a loss on the year's working of ..e9 us. sd. The expendi- ture was 4;10 below the average for the past five years. The balance sheet was adopted.

LATEST CAT STORY. The latest •animal story from the other side of the Atlantic (says the " Westminster Gazette") concerns a cat, belonging to Major Cannon, of the Quartermaster's Department at Governors' Island. Commodore Nutt, as the cat is called, was sentenced to lianishment from the island for making the squirrels his favourite diet. The cut was excqedingly wily in its methods. Ile would roll a nut near the squirrels' haunts, and then lie in wait for a victim. The Commodore was at length caught in the act, and reported to headquarters. As the outcome of the court-martial held upon his pet, Major Cannon was notified that Commo- dore Nutt would have to leave the island, or else be provided with a bell. The bell was fixed to the cat at once, and the animal had perforce to go about the island tinking like a bell-weatlmr. But the squirrels continued to die as before, and a watch was set upon the crafty Commodore. He was spotted by a sentry among the 'trees, stalking his prey on three feet. His left forefoot clutched the bell at his neck! When that bell tinkled it tolled the death-knell of yet another squirrel: That settled the tenure of Mr. Nutt's lease of life on Gov- ernors' Island. He was brought from his happy hunting-ground at the end of last month and east into outer darkness, joining the great army of unemployed cats in New York City.

RUGBY FOOTBALL.

ANNUAL MEETING OF THE LANCASHIRE

COUNTY UNION.

At the annual general meeting, of the Lanca- shire County Rugby Football Union, held at Manchester this week, Mr. J. Platt (Oldham) presiding, the balance-sheet for the past season, showing .the satisfactory balance ii . hand of .1200, was adopted.

Shield Competition Unpopular.

. . An important item on the agenda was the consideration of the Shield Competition. The Chairman explained that the competi- tion was becoming more and more unpopular every year, and last year the entries were only seven. The committee felt that unless a keener interest could be taken in the competition it was useless to keep it going.. Mr. J. Counsell (Wigan) thought otherwise. He had attended a meeting- of the Wigan High- field Club about a fortnight ago, and the mem- bers were looking forward to the continuance of the competition. • • -

_ It was decided that unless eight entrants be received for the competition nest season it should be discontinued.

Councillor Osborne on the Committee.

The following were elected on the committee : Messrs. W. M. Gabbott (Barrow), If. Rebbitt (Broughton Rangers), R. Gale (Leigh), J. Val- entine (Manchester and District League), j. Platt (Oldham), G. F. Hutchins (Oldham and District League), G. R. Dex (Rochdale Hornets), H. Yates (Runcorn), J. Gough (Salford), J. H. Houghton (St. Helens), J. W. anSedhAol.esii.(LSawii): ton), E. Osborne (Warrington), J. IL Smith (Widnes), J. Counsell (Wigan and District League).

===== 0002601_19140620_art0281.txt | included=true | included =====
Rules Altered.

Rule 12 of the Senior Cup Competition was amended to read: "All clubs shall be required to send in by October 1 a list of duly qualified players." Rule 11. of the Junior Cup Competition was amended as follows: "All clubs shall be re- quired to send to their opponents at least three days before each tie a list of bona-fide playing members, and from such list shall teams be made up. No player shall play for more than one club in the same season's cup tie. No player shall be eligible to take part in this com- petition who is not registered previous to the date of the third round."

Officials for Next Season. A meeting of the committee was subsequently held and the following officials were elected for the year: President. Mr. J. Platt (Oldham); vice-president, Mr. H. Rebbitt (Eroughton Ran- gers); hon. treasurer, Mr. J. H. Smith (Wid- nes); hon. secretary, Mr. J. H. Houghton (St. Helens). Emergency Committee: Messrs. J. Platt (Oldham), H. Rebbitt (Broughton Ran- gers). J. H. Smith (Widnes), J. H. Houghton (St. Helens), and J. Counsell (Wigan).

Junior Cup Dates. The Lancashire Junior Cup dates were fixed as follows: First round, October 10; second round, November 21; third round, January 16; fourth round, February 13; semi-final, March 6; final tie. April 5.

The dates for county matches were left in the hands of the secretary to arrange.

JUNIOR LEAGUE FORMED. On Wednesday a meeting was held in Rylands' Recreation Clubroom, Gorsey-lane, to consider the advisability of forming a Warrington Junior League, under the Northern Rugby Union. It was resolved to form such a League, with a ten-mile radius. The secretary was asked to write to different clubs asking them to consider the matter. The next meeting will be held at the same place next Friday at 7.30 p.m. Mr. .1. Sheader, 220, Manchester-road, :was elected secretary pro tem. All communications must be sent to him.

===== 0002601_19140620_art0282.txt | included=true | included =====
Be a Man.

There is something despicable in a strong, healthy young man who is continually whining over his lot in life, excusing indifference and inaction because of hard luck or some cruel fate which has put stumbling-blocks in his way. No matter , what your environment, or what you may be called upon to go through, face life like a man, without whining. Turn your face to the sun, your back to the shadows, and look- the world in the face without wincing. Make the most of your circumstances. See the beauties in it and not the ugly features. This is the way to improve an unfortunate environment.

OUR ANNUAL REST. Tradesmen and Railway Companies Contrive to Make us Happy, "EXAMINER "ADVERTISERS' EFFORTS. Last week we drew attention to the advan- tages of an annual holiday and the facilities that exist to allow nearly all to gratify their desire for change4and rest. To-day we give in some detail the arrangements that are made in various directions to cater for us in the holiday season. TRADESMEN'S SPECIAL HOLIDAY ANNOUNCEAIENTS. • W. HODGKINSON, LTD. The spacious windows of this noted firm in Bridge-street bear emphatic evidence of the proximity of the holiday season. Dainty, yet inexpensive, dresses, fascinating millinery, sports coats in Shantung and other smart materials, neckwear and gloves—all these, and all other requisites are there in profusion at prices that are remarkably low. The travelling trunks stocked by Hodgkinson's are such as to meet every possible require- ment. R. ROBERTS and SON 1, Bootmakers, 19, Bridge-street, are specialising in "Lotus" Shoes, which represent the best and smartest in ladies' footwear; and the ever-in- creasing demand proves the Merit of the goods. All are of the highest quality, and leave noth- ing to be desired so far as smartness goes. The "Delta" Shoe may safely be claimed to be remarkable value for a 10s. J. shoe. It is stocked in many styles, shapes and half-sizes. The new "Greek" Court shoe is particularly smart for holiday wear. Every pair is guaran- teed. With regard to gentlemen's wear, the cele- brated "K" boots and shoes more than ever retain their great popularity, and without doubt the "Exact" "K" in box calf, glace kid and willow calf is the best 16s. 9d. boot on the market to-day. The firm also stock the "Delta." " Footform," " Bective," and "Keltic " gentlemen's goods.' and they, along with the " Lotus " and "Delta" in ladies' goods, are the pick of the English market. Patrons may be assured that with a sixty years' reputation for quality to maintain, this established firm will only show goods which will enhance their reputation. With the various sizes, fittings, and shapes, E. R. and Son can give the utmost satisfaction in regard to "fit" —in fact, no foot is too difficult for them to deal with. C. MOUNFIELD. "Everything for Men" is the holiday motto of C. Mounfield. A glance at the windows of his attractive Premises opposite Parr's Bank will convince you of this, and the ladies, by the way, are not forgotten. To enumerate the many necessary commodities offered would exhaust our , space. Sufficient for us to say that by going farther you may fare much worse—a somewhat hack- neyed proverb, perhaps, but none the le gg true. No man who really prides himself ,upon his per- sonal appearance can afford to miss the oppor- tunity of 'a real holiday rig-out such as Mona- field's can supply—a real outfit—embracing everything stylish and up-to-date at a price to fit the purse. To be well dressed Adds zest to pleasure, and the man Who consults Mounfield is the man who carries the mark of distinction which places him above the "ordinary. LATCHFORD LAUNDRY. A few years ago Walking Day and holiday time was a period of heavy expense and worry- ing responsibilities for housewives and hus- bands. Every garment must bo new, and if the expense was too great to be met, as was often the ease where the family was numerous, the result was disappointed and miserable children and the consequent irritability Of parents. Nowadays, however, all this is changed. The Latchford Steam Laundry, Thelwall-lane, has made it possible for everyone to be satisfied and smart. The Special Dry Cleaning Process in- troduced by this up-to-date firm turns out every article in superior manner—frocks, ' dresses, laces and all other dainty fabrics are specially treated and delivered entirely renovated, and in every respect equal to new. The cost, when, compared with the results, is trifling, and well within the reach of all. Patrons may depend upon the best service in every department of this high-class laundry. Special terms are quoted for large family wash- ing. Carpets and tapestries are thoroughly cleaned by new vacuum process. BAG AND BAGGAGE. Hints to Ensure Contfort and Peace 4)1 Mind. The approach of the holiday season brings the question of luggage prominently before us. One of the most serious problems confronting the traveller is how to compress into as small a compass as possible all the articles he wants to take away, and the next is how to get the various packages to his destination. In connection with the latter question, the railway companies have done a great deal to- wards making the holiday-seeker happy on his journeys by relieving him of the troUble of looking after his baggage. The luggage in ad- vance system is one of the greatest boons ever conferred upon the travelling public. A note to the company brings the collecting van to your door, and for an insignificant sum your impedimenta is conveyed to your hotel or lodg- ings to await your arrival. A few years ago the family's annual trip to the sea was a perfect nightmare to the father of the flock. There was the cab to the station, tips to the porters, fran- tic rushings up and down the train to see that nothing has been left on the platform, the same performance repeated at each junction, aid by the time the destination was reached—probably Continued on Page 4.

A West Country Holiday. THERE is a subtle charm about the beautiful West of England— The Cornish Riviera or Glorious Devon—that is felt nowhere else in the Kingdom. It is a charm that the North-countryman will especially appreciate because he will contrast the scenic splendour of the West with that of the liorth. He will enjoy the mild, equable climate of the West—its heritage. And while his health is being benefited his interest will be maintained in the shire which gave birth to the Elizabethan Sea Kings, or in the "Delectable Duchy." "Holiday Haunts." The popular holiday guide issued by the Great Western Railway tells all about the charming resorts served by the system. Besides these two counties the book illustrates and describes resorts in Somerset, Dorset, North • and South Vliates, Ireland, etc. Copies are obtainable at the Companies' loocal Agency, or from the Superintendent of the Line, Paddington Station, London, W.
